187:## #12 — `SolveSym3` robustness + compile reassociation (replaces plan:215–216, plan:322, plan:350, plan T2)
196:def solve_constrained_xz(M00, M02, M22, Px, Pz):
204:    reg = where(kappa > KAPPA_MAX_F32, EPS_SPD*tr, 0.0)          # KAPPA_MAX_F32 = 1e6
206:    inv = 1.0 / where(abs(d) > EPS_REL*tr*tr, d, EPS_REL*tr*tr)  # relative det guard, not absolute 1e-12
213:- **Retained donor cofactor form** (`solve_sym3_donor`) reproduces SwimEval.cs:1151–1166 op-order and the absolute `|det|<1e-12` fallback **byte-for-byte**, used **only** to match gain0/bug-inert step-trace fixtures. Production uses `solve_constrained_xz`. `test_solve.py::donor_conformance` asserts they agree on well-conditioned bodies and documents the (intended) divergence where the donor is numerically wrong.
217:- **Compile-vs-eager parity** (plan:350 "force/solve-bound" claim depends on this): the "exact donor op-order" determinism claim requires `torch.compile` not reassociate the cofactor arithmetic. Enforce via `test_solve.py::compile_parity` — assert `max_abs(Δ)==0` between r0-eager and r1/r2-compiled `solve_sym3_donor` on the extreme corpus. **If parity fails, `solve_sym3_donor` is emitted as a fixed-lowering custom op (`torch.library`) or run in an eager island outside the compiled graph**, and that cost is reported in the §2.7 bench. `solve_constrained_xz` (production) is SPD-robust and does not require bit-exact donor op-order, so it may compile freely; only the *conformance* solve needs the no-reassociation boundary.
219:**Plan seam signatures amended** (plan:215–216): `solve_sym3` → two functions — `solve_sym3_donor(m00..m22, rhs)` (donor cofactor op-order EXACTLY, gain0 conformance only) and `solve_constrained_xz(M00,M02,M22, Px,Pz)` (SPD-robust production, scale-relative regularization). Plan:322 degenerate-guard item updated: guards are `torch.where` on **scale-relative** thresholds, never absolute `1e-12`, never dropped rows.
228:- plan:307 → `quat_inv = conj/‖q‖²` (#10).
230:- plan:215–216,322,350,377 (T2) → `solve_sym3_donor`/`solve_constrained_xz` split, extreme corpus, compile-parity (#12).
245:### Finding #3 — Deterministic segmented reduction
255:def masked_segment_sum(vals: Tensor,          # [B, S_max, *F]  finite in ALL lanes
266:- Axial form drag: `fDrag = masked_segment_sum(quat_rotate(rot, floc), seg_mask)` → `[B,3]` (donor `SwimEval.cs:790`).
267:- Dissipated power: `wDrag = masked_segment_sum(relu(-(fworld*uj).sum(-1)), seg_mask)` → `[B]` (`SwimEval.cs:791`).
268:- Six added-mass entries: each `m_kl = masked_segment_sum(ma * col_k * col_l, seg_mask)` → `[B]`, then add the structural diagonal `mbody*250` **after** the reduction (`SwimEval.cs:771,796–801`; keeps the ×250 on struct-only, watch-item plan:318).
269:- `mbody = masked_segment_sum(seg_mass, seg_mask)`.
273:1. `torch.sum` along a **fixed dimension** is on PyTorch's list of operations that have a deterministic implementation; the nondeterministic-on-CUDA operations are precisely the atomic scatters (`index_add`, `scatter_add`, `bincount`, `index_put(accumulate=True)`) — none of which appear anywhere in the hot path now. Under `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True, warn_only=False)` (plan:222) a residual nondeterministic op would *raise*, not silently pass.
277:**Finite-padding invariant (must hold or masking silently corrupts):** `vals` must be finite in *padded* lanes too, because `0.0 * NaN = NaN` would leak through the multiply. Every per-segment computation writes a neutral value into padded lanes: padded `seg_mass=0`, `seg_ma=0`, and any lane produced by a division/`quat` op is guarded so padded rows carry finite defaults (this dovetails with the #9 sentinel-row fix for root/empty gathers). Debug builds assert `torch.isfinite(vals).all()` before every `masked_segment_sum`; the mask is applied *before* the sum, never after.
282:- plan:60 → `reduce.py  # masked_segment_sum(vals[B,S_max,*],seg_mask[B,S_max]) → axis-sum over segment dim; NO scatter, NO body_id`
283:- plan:216 signature → `def masked_segment_sum(vals, seg_mask) -> Tensor  # (vals*mask).sum(dim=1); deterministic axis reduction`
285:- plan:807 → "`reduce.py::masked_segment_sum` (deterministic masked axis-sum; no atomics, no CSR)."
291:### Finding #12 (determinism half) — compile / reassociation reproducibility
322:### Finding #13 — RNG stream stability (counter-based keyed stateless RNG)
333:(seed, step, stable_entity_id, gene_iid, event_kind, draw_index)
345:| `draw_index` | block index within one draw | 8 | counter |
349:`event_kind` enum (reserved, extensible to 256): `0 REPRO_ASEXUAL_SELECT`, `1 STRUCTURAL_ADD_NODE`, `2 STRUCTURAL_ADD_EDGE`, `3 STRUCTURAL_DELETE`, `4 PARAM_JITTER`, `5 MUTATION_BERNOULLI`, `6 MATE_CROSSOVER_MASK`, `7 MATE_PARTNER_SELECT`, `8 DEVELOP_STOCHASTIC` (reserved), `9 FIELD_STOCHASTIC` (reserved), … A draw needing more than 128 output bits increments `draw_index` (e.g. a normal via Box–Muller uses `draw_index` 0 for two uniforms; a rejection-sampled draw increments until accept).
361:def _mulhilo(a, b):                          # a,b: int64 tensors of uint32 values
367:        hi0, lo0 = _mulhilo(c0, _M0)
368:        hi1, lo1 = _mulhilo(c2, _M1)
373:def keyed_bits(seed, step, eid, gene_iid, event_kind, draw_index):  # tensors -> 4x[B] u32
374:    _assert_field_widths(step, eid, gene_iid, event_kind, draw_index)
378:    c3 =  (gene_iid >> 8) & 0xFFFF | (event_kind << 16) | (draw_index << 24)
395:**Per-world monotone allocator** `next_eid [W] i64` (part of the snapshot, §13.5). Birth assignment is deterministic and batched — no atomics, no host loop:
400:eid    = next_eid.unsqueeze(1) + rank                             # contiguous ids, slot-order
403:next_eid  = next_eid + newborn_mask.sum(dim=1)                    # advance allocator
412:> **I-GENOME-5 (counter-based keyed RNG; §2.7).** The simulation draws no random numbers from a sequential stream. Every draw is produced by `PRF(key, counter)` keyed by `(seed, step, stable_entity_id, gene_iid, event_kind, draw_index)`. Because a draw is *addressed by content* rather than consumed by position, an inert gene occupies no stream position; activating a previously inert gene introduces only that gene's own draws (keyed by its stable `gene_iid`, I-GENOME-4) and shifts no other gene's or organism's draws. Slot recycling, compaction, birth, and death cannot re-baseline determinism because organism streams are keyed by never-reused `stable_id`, not by slot index or draw order. There is no manifest and no sequential draw counter.
418:The authoritative snapshot must carry everything the keyed RNG and allocator depend on. `ColonyState` gains `stable_id`, `parent_id`; the versioned `SimulationSnapshot` (the #14-owned object) additionally carries `next_eid[W]`, the innovation registry (`next_iid` + within-generation structural-key cache, design:677), the run `seed`, and `SimClock.step`. With those, `keyed_bits` is fully reconstructible → replay is bit-identical. (Snapshot schema itself is #14's deliverable; this fix only enumerates the RNG/allocator fields it must include.)
451:**Files/sections this fix rewrites:** plan:9 (contract wording), plan:56 & 60 (module comments), plan:105 (test file), plan:165–181 (`ColonyState` + `stable_id`/`parent_id`), plan:213–226 (§1.7 determinism rules), plan:287–293 (§2.1 layout → `[B,S_max]`+`seg_mask`), plan:307–308 (`seg_sum` → `masked_segment_sum`), plan:343–344 (gate (a) tests), plan:661–663 (§5.3 determinism plan), plan:770 (R5), plan:807 (§7 numerics tasks); design:634 (I-GENOME-5), design:721 (mutation-operator manifest note), design:1194 (RK-6). The `numerics/reduce.py::segment_index_add` and `numerics/rng.py::RngManifest` symbols are **deleted**; `masked_segment_sum` and `keyed_bits`/Philox replace them.
462:These two fixes are coupled: #14's checkpoint cannot be defined until #4 fixes what the authoritative in-memory state *is*. I resolve #4 first (canonical padded layout + static lifecycle), then #14 (`SimulationSnapshot` over that state).
466:## Finding #4 — "Static-shape architecture contradicts the flattened live-segment layout"
499:| `seg_parent_local` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | i16 | **parent index within the SAME creature's `S_max` axis**, ∈ `[0,S_max)`; root → own index (self-parent, `seg_hasJoint=False`) |
504:**Key change vs the flattened design:** `seg_parent_local` is now a **creature-local** index into the `S_max` axis, not a global `body_base + local_parent`. All parent gathers in the pose pass (`torch.gather` along `SEG_AXIS`) are therefore in-range `[0,S_max)` and can never read another creature's segment. This eliminates `body_ptr`/`body_base`/`body_id` maintenance entirely — `body_id` no longer exists because segments are indexed by their `(world, creature, slot)` position, not by a flat id. (The root-`-1`/empty-body gather hazard of #9 is handled by the self-parent + `seg_hasJoint=False` convention here and completed in the #9 cluster.)
515:`torch.sum` over a fixed contiguous axis is a deterministic pairwise-tree reduction under `use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`; no sanctioned-wrapper indirection is needed. `numerics/reduce.py::segment_index_add` (plan:60,216,223) is **deleted**; the Grep-rule scaffold (plan:223) is replaced by an AST/import guard asserting the hot loop contains no `index_add_`/`scatter_add_` at all, which is now enforceable because the code genuinely contains none.
529:| `struct_N` | `[W,N_cap]` | **f64** | structural nutrient [mol] — f64 reservoir per #5 |
566:stable_id  = torch.where(claim, next_stable_id + global_ord, stable_id)   # next_stable_id: host i64
568:next_stable_id += int(n_birth_w.sum())                  # the ONLY host-side scalar advanced
570:`next_stable_id` is the free-list/allocator state (a single i64 counter) — it, plus the `generation` array, fully captures allocator status; the free-slot *list* itself is never stored because it is recomputed from `alive` each step. This is what the plan's "precomputed free-slot list (never atomic append)" (plan:167) becomes: a per-step `cumsum` over `~alive`, not a persisted mutable list.
576:A capture holds iff every replayed op has fixed shapes, fixed addresses, and no host synchronization. Under §4.2–4.4: (1) all tensors are `Config`-constant `[W,N_cap,·]`/`[W,N_cap,S_max,·]`, allocated once at `reset`; (2) birth/death are `where`/`gather`/`cumsum` over those buffers — no resize, no `index_select`, no `.item()` inside the step; (3) `S_max` and `N_cap` are frozen in `Config`, so the `[B,S_max]` view is address-stable. The only host-side scalar (`next_stable_id`) is advanced **outside** the captured region (it feeds the *next* step's payload build), so it does not break capture. `n_birth_w` overflow is resolved by `minimum` on-device, not a Python branch. Gate-(a) `churn_deterministic` (plan:344) and G-SCAF-4 (plan:264) are therefore satisfiable by construction.
582:**Plan edits for #4:** rewrite §2.1 lines 287–293 (segment layout → `[W,N_cap,S_max]`+`seg_mask`, delete `[S_total]`/CSR/`body_id`, invert the padded/flattened framing); update §1.5 lines 165–181 (add `stable_id`/`parent_id`/`generation`, drop `body_ptr`, promote `energy`/`struct_N` to f64); update §1.6 line 201 (`DevelopedBody` → padded `[B,S_max]`); delete `numerics/reduce.py::segment_index_add` (lines 60,216,223) and rewrite plan:223 as an AST guard; rewrite `spikeswim/churn.py` note (line 99) to the §4.4 recycle-by-`cumsum` scheme.
586:## Finding #14 — "Replay from ColonyState alone is false"
590:**Resolution.** Define a **versioned `SimulationSnapshot`** as the *complete* checkpoint. `ColonyState` is explicitly re-scoped to "the creature-state subset, not a checkpoint." Counter-based keyed RNG (#13) makes this dramatically simpler: there is **no PRNG internal buffer to serialize** — the entire RNG state is `(master_seed, step, next_stable_id)`, all of which live in Config/clock/allocator. This is called out as the payoff of the #13 decision.
592:### 14.1 `SimulationSnapshot` — the complete authoritative state (new `core/snapshot.py`)
605:class SimulationSnapshot:
611:    reservoirs:  dict            # f64: Nd,Bp,Bd,Bm [W,G,G,B]; struct_N,energy [W,N_cap]; Sed [W,G,G];
613:    allocator:   dict            # {next_stable_id:i64}  (free-list is recomputed from alive, §4.4)
625:| RNG states | `rng` + `clock.step` + `allocator.next_stable_id` (counter-based ⇒ no buffer) |
626:| stable IDs / free-list | `colony.stable_id/parent_id/generation` + `allocator.next_stable_id` |
634:- **Format:** tensors → a single `tensors.safetensors` (typed, deterministic, no pickle, CPU-resident); scalars/registries/config → `meta.json`; `header.json` alongside. Directory or zip. `torch.save`/pickle is forbidden (nondeterministic dict ordering, arbitrary-code hazard).
636:- **API** (`core/snapshot.py`):
638:  def save(snap: SimulationSnapshot, path: Path) -> None
639:  def load(path: Path) -> SimulationSnapshot        # raises on header.schema_version major mismatch
641:  def Colony.snapshot(self) -> SimulationSnapshot    # gather complete state
642:  def Colony.restore(self, snap: SimulationSnapshot) -> None   # rehydrate ALL of the above
644:  `Colony.load(ColonyState)` (plan:198) is **removed** and replaced by `Colony.restore(SimulationSnapshot)`. `ColonyState.serialize/load` (plan:86) survives only as the creature-subset (de)serializer used *inside* `snapshot()/restore()`, and its docstring is changed to "creature-state subset — NOT a checkpoint; see `SimulationSnapshot`."
676:**Plan edits for #14:** add `core/snapshot.py` to the tree (plan:82–95) and `tests/test_snapshot.py`; change plan:198 `Colony.load(s)` → `Colony.restore(snap)` + `Colony.snapshot()`; re-scope `core/state.py` docstring (plan:86); rewrite S7 components (plan:602) and acceptance (plan:604) to read "replay-from-checkpoint from a serialized **`SimulationSnapshot`**" and "reproduces a checkpointed run from a **`SimulationSnapshot`** (not `ColonyState`) alone"; mirror the same wording in design:602–604; add the round-trip + insufficiency gates to §5.2 (plan:646) and the S7 row.

805:`d_dd·Bp` (crowding) is what gives the crash its bite: at the bloom peak `Bp` is large, mortality is super-linear, biomass collapses even as production has already stalled from `Nd` exhaustion. `M_p` feeds `Bd` → S1.8 remin returns `(1−BGE)` of it to `Nd` (deep) → S1.10 mixing lifts it → **damped re-bloom → steady standing stock**. This is genuine bloom-and-crash, fully conserved, **no cap knob** — the crash is a real loss flux, not a ceiling. `m_resp, d0, d_dd` are frozen *rate anchors* (§1.2), never retuned to hit a target biomass; a failure to stabilize is a diagnostic, not a tuning target (§6.8, §6.9).

951:## Finding #6 — Throughput gate is arbitrary, contradictory, not end-to-end
979:> **S0 authorizes exactly one thing: that the frozen-heading locomotion *kernel* is fast enough to be a viable component of the whole tick — a NECESSARY, not sufficient, condition.** S0 does **not** measure or authorize the end-to-end tick. The end-to-end throughput thesis is decided at a later named gate **G-E2E** (§6.5), post-S2/S3, once StepLive + development + at least one field + spatial hash + feeding exist.
985:                  # single heaviest per-creature subsystem; assumption VALIDATED at G-E2E
1005:> **(d) Throughput** — `bench.py`→`test_throughput.py::floor`: at the near-term single-world batch `B=W·N_cap` (H1/H2), the best `(device,rung)` clears **F_loco_S0 = 1.4e8 creature-steps/s** (derived §6.2–6.3; *not* `3.07e7×10`). Report `B*` (CPU↔GPU crossover) and the per-device curve across the B-ladder; report H1/H0 heterogeneity tax and padded-mask tax. Profiler (winning + any falsifier-tripped cell only) must show **force/solve-bound, not pose-scan/reduction-bound**. **S0 authorizes locomotion-kernel feasibility only; end-to-end authorization is deferred to G-E2E (§6.5).**
1007:### 6.5 NEW named later gate — **G-E2E** (add to §4/§6 milestones, gates post-S2, re-confirmed post-S3/S4)
1009:> **G-E2E (end-to-end throughput authorization).** *Dependency: S2 StepLive + genome development + ≥1 field (S1) + spatial hash + S3 feeding stub.* Benchmark **one whole tick** (`Colony.step`: develop-if-dirty → field sample/advect → spatial hash rebuild → StepLive → feeding → metabolism → churn → telemetry) at `N_pop=1000`, one world, best `(device,rung)`. **Floor: `F_sci = 2.31e7` creature-steps/s (≈193× realtime).** Also emits the *measured* `φ_loco` (locomotion share of the tick); if `φ_loco > 0.5`, the S0 gate was optimistic and G-E2E is the binding authority; if `< 0.5`, S0's floor was conservative (safe). **This — not S0 — authorizes the full vectorization thesis.** A NO-GO here escalates the language/kernel ladder (§5.8) on the *dominant* subsystem the profiler names, which may be fields or hashing, not locomotion.

1178:> **Critical path:** Scaffold → S0 → S1 → S2 → **S3**.    470	**Resolution (adopts the pre-decided cross-cutting decision).** The canonical layout is **fixed `[B, S_max]`-padded segment tensors + a boolean `seg_mask`**, with `B = W·N_cap`. `S_total` is deleted from the design. CSR/flattened storage is demoted to a *deferred, measured* optimization (the exact inverse of the plan's current "padded is only an ablation" stance). This is what kills #3 as well: per-body reductions become plain **masked axis-sums over the `S_max` axis**, `(vals * seg_mask).sum(dim=SEG_AXIS)` — a fixed-shape contiguous-axis tree reduction, deterministic by construction, no `index_add_`, no `scatter_add_`, no atomics, no duplicate-`body_id` destinations. (The detailed determinism argument for the reduction primitive is owned by the #3 cluster; this cluster supplies the layout that makes it a trivial `sum`.)
   471	
   472	### 4.1 Canonical shape constants (Config, frozen)
   473	
   474	| symbol | value | meaning | source |
   475	|---|---|---|---|
   476	| `W` | Config.w | worlds | — |
   477	| `N_cap` | Config.n_cap (1024 default) | per-world creature capacity | plan:187 |
   478	| `S_max` | **16** | per-creature **physics** segment capacity | donor physics cap, `SwimEval` PropWalk `:442–460` |
   479	| `B` | `W·N_cap` | flattened creature index for the kernel | plan:288 |
   480	| `L` | 6 | development / pose depth passes | design:652, plan:303 |
   481	
   482	`S_max = 16` is the *developed-body / physics* capacity (what `swim_step` consumes). It is distinct from the **genotype** capacities `N_max = 24`, `E_max = 48` (design:652), which live at the genetics layer and are never touched by the hot physics loop. Development (genetics→DevelopedBody) is the pure fixed-shape map that fills `[·, S_max, ·]` from `[·, N_max, ·]` (design:630, I-GENOME-1); it can emit at most `S_max` segments and masks the rest. There is **no** `S_max_dev=32` in the physics body — the design's `S_max=32` is genotype post-mirror node capacity, a different tensor at a different layer; this reconciliation removes that ambiguity.
   483	
   484	### 4.2 Segment layout — REPLACES plan §2.1 lines 287–293 and §1.6 line 201
   485	
   486	Segment tensors are canonically stored `(W, N_cap, S_max, …)` and viewed as `[B, S_max, …]` for the kernel (a reshape, never a copy — `B` and `S_max` are compile-time constants so the view is graph-stable).
   487	
   488	| tensor | shape | dtype | notes |
   489	|---|---|---|---|
   490	| `seg_mask` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | bool | **sole source of truth for segment existence**; padded slots `False` |
   491	| `seg_localPos` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,3]` | f32 | rest position in parent frame [m] |
   492	| `seg_localRot` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,4]` | f32 | rest orientation quat (parent frame) |
   493	| `seg_abc` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,3]` | f32 | ellipsoid semi-axes [m] |
   494	| `seg_mass` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | f32 | box or ellipsoid per gain-stage (§2.0) |
   495	| `seg_areaZ` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | f32 | axial cross-section [m²] |
   496	| `seg_ma` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,3]` | f32 | Lamb added-mass per axis (cast f64→f32 after LambK, per #11) |
   497	| `seg_ampDeg,seg_phase,seg_c` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | f32 | gait actuation + half-length |
   498	| `seg_isTail,seg_hasJoint` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | bool | tail selection / hinge presence |
   499	| `seg_parent_local` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | i16 | **parent index within the SAME creature's `S_max` axis**, ∈ `[0,S_max)`; root → own index (self-parent, `seg_hasJoint=False`) |
   500	| `seg_depth` | `[W,N_cap,S_max]` | i8 | DFS depth ∈ `[0,L)`; padded → `-1` (never selected) |
   501	| working: `pos,prev_pos` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,3]` | f32 | pose-pass scratch |
   502	| working: `rot` | `[W,N_cap,S_max,4]` | f32 | pose-pass scratch |
   503	
   504	**Key change vs the flattened design:** `seg_parent_local` is now a **creature-local** index into the `S_max` axis, not a global `body_base + local_parent`. All parent gathers in the pose pass (`torch.gather` along `SEG_AXIS`) are therefore in-range `[0,S_max)` and can never read another creature's segment. This eliminates `body_ptr`/`body_base`/`body_id` maintenance entirely — `body_id` no longer exists because segments are indexed by their `(world, creature, slot)` position, not by a flat id. (The root-`-1`/empty-body gather hazard of #9 is handled by the self-parent + `seg_hasJoint=False` convention here and completed in the #9 cluster.)
   505	
   506	**Per-body reductions (masked axis-sum, replacing every `segment_index_add`):**
   507	```
   508	SEG_AXIS = 2
   509	m_body   = (seg_mass * seg_mask).sum(SEG_AXIS)                 # [W,N_cap]
   510	# M_eff assembly (donor S9): per-seg outer products, masked, summed over S_max
   511	col_x = quat_rotate(rot, x_hat)                               # [W,N_cap,S_max,3]
   512	m00   = (seg_ma[...,0] * col_x[...,0]*col_x[...,0] * seg_mask).sum(SEG_AXIS)   # etc. for m01..m22
   513	fDrag = (quat_rotate(rot, floc) * seg_mask.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(SEG_AXIS)        # [W,N_cap,3]
   514	```
   515	`torch.sum` over a fixed contiguous axis is a deterministic pairwise-tree reduction under `use_deterministic_algorithms(True)`; no sanctioned-wrapper indirection is needed. `numerics/reduce.py::segment_index_add` (plan:60,216,223) is **deleted**; the Grep-rule scaffold (plan:223) is replaced by an AST/import guard asserting the hot loop contains no `index_add_`/`scatter_add_` at all, which is now enforceable because the code genuinely contains none.
   516	
   517	### 4.3 Creature layout — REVISES plan §1.5 (lines 165–181)
   518	
   519	The `ColonyState` table gains the identity/lifecycle fields required by #13 (stable RNG keying) and #14 (checkpoint), and **drops the flattened `body_ptr`** (segments are co-indexed with creatures now; only `genome_ptr` into the `[P,…]` genotype pool remains):
   520	
   521	| tensor | shape | dtype | notes |
   522	|---|---|---|---|
   523	| `alive` | `[W,N_cap]` | bool | sole source of truth for creature existence |
   524	| `stable_id` | `[W,N_cap]` | i64 | **monotonic, never reused** (per-run); 0 = never-allocated sentinel |
   525	| `parent_id` | `[W,N_cap]` | i64 | `stable_id` of parent (0 for founders) |
   526	| `generation` | `[W,N_cap]` | i32 | slot-reuse counter (ABA guard / lineage debug) |
   527	| `pos,heading,lin_vel,ang_vel` | as plan:172–175 | f32 | physics state |
   528	| `energy` | `[W,N_cap]` | **f64** | metabolic reserve [J] — promoted to f64 per #5 (reservoir) |
   529	| `struct_N` | `[W,N_cap]` | **f64** | structural nutrient [mol] — f64 reservoir per #5 |
   530	| `genome_ptr` | `[W,N_cap]` | i64 | → row in `[P,…]` genotype pool |
   531	| `age` | `[W,N_cap]` | f64 | [s] |
   532	| `species_tag` | `[W,N_cap]` | i64 | observational only (never gates mating) |
   533	
   534	(`body_ptr` removed; the developed-body segment tensors of §4.2 are the body, co-indexed `[W,N_cap,S_max,…]`.)
   535	
   536	### 4.4 Static-shape lifecycle (birth / death / refill) — REPLACES the "precomputed free-slot list / append" text at plan:167, and `spikeswim/churn.py` semantics at plan:99
   537	
   538	**Invariant:** every operation is a `where`/`gather`/`cumsum` over the fixed `[W,N_cap,…]` / `[W,N_cap,S_max,…]` buffers. No tensor is ever resized, reallocated, `index_select`-ed, or compacted. Therefore a single captured CUDA graph replays every tick (§4.5).
   539	
   540	**Death** (masked clear, deterministic):
   541	```
   542	alive = alive & ~die_mask          # [W,N_cap]; dead slots' data left stale, masked out everywhere
   543	```
   544	No compaction, ever. Staleness is safe because every reduction multiplies by `alive` (creature axis) and `seg_mask` (segment axis).
   545	
   546	**Birth / dead-slot recycling** (deterministic, no atomics, static shape):
   547	```
   548	free       = ~alive                                    # [W,N_cap]
   549	free_rank  = torch.cumsum(free.to(i32), dim=1) - 1     # k-th free slot in world w has rank k
   550	n_free_w   = free.sum(1)                                # [W]
   551	n_birth_w  = torch.minimum(request_w, n_free_w)         # overflow deterministically dropped, logged
   552	claim      = free & (free_rank < n_birth_w[:,None])     # [W,N_cap] slots to fill this step
   553	
   554	# child payload is prebuilt indexed by BIRTH ORDINAL (0..n_birth_w) per world, padded to N_cap:
   555	#   payload_*[w, ord, ...]  (parent lookups are gathers → deterministic)
   556	# free_rank is a bijection claimed-slot ↔ ordinal, so scatter-by-gather:
   557	new_field  = torch.gather(payload_field, 1, free_rank.unsqueeze(-1).expand_as(field))
   558	field      = torch.where(claim.unsqueeze(-1), new_field, field)   # per creature/segment tensor
   559	alive      = alive | claim
   560	generation = generation + claim.to(i32)                 # slot reuse bumps generation
   561	```
   562	**Stable-id assignment** (monotonic, never reused; deterministic across worlds by `(world, free_rank)` order):
   563	```
   564	world_off  = torch.cumsum(n_birth_w, 0) - n_birth_w     # [W] global-ordinal base per world
   565	global_ord = world_off[:,None] + free_rank              # valid where claim
   566	stable_id  = torch.where(claim, next_stable_id + global_ord, stable_id)   # next_stable_id: host i64
   567	parent_id  = torch.where(claim, gather(parent_stable_id_by_ord, free_rank), parent_id)
   568	next_stable_id += int(n_birth_w.sum())                  # the ONLY host-side scalar advanced
   569	```
   570	`next_stable_id` is the free-list/allocator state (a single i64 counter) — it, plus the `generation` array, fully captures allocator status; the free-slot *list* itself is never stored because it is recomputed from `alive` each step. This is what the plan's "precomputed free-slot list (never atomic append)" (plan:167) becomes: a per-step `cumsum` over `~alive`, not a persisted mutable list.
   571	
   572	Developed-body segment slots of a newly-claimed creature are filled the same way (`gather` the child's developed body along the `S_max` axis, `where` on `claim`), so `seg_mask` for a reused slot is overwritten wholesale — no residue from the previous tenant leaks into a masked sum.
   573	
   574	### 4.5 CUDA-graph-capture argument (makes G-SCAF-4 / gate (a) real)
   575	
   576	A capture holds iff every replayed op has fixed shapes, fixed addresses, and no host synchronization. Under §4.2–4.4: (1) all tensors are `Config`-constant `[W,N_cap,·]`/`[W,N_cap,S_max,·]`, allocated once at `reset`; (2) birth/death are `where`/`gather`/`cumsum` over those buffers — no resize, no `index_select`, no `.item()` inside the step; (3) `S_max` and `N_cap` are frozen in `Config`, so the `[B,S_max]` view is address-stable. The only host-side scalar (`next_stable_id`) is advanced **outside** the captured region (it feeds the *next* step's payload build), so it does not break capture. `n_birth_w` overflow is resolved by `minimum` on-device, not a Python branch. Gate-(a) `churn_deterministic` (plan:344) and G-SCAF-4 (plan:264) are therefore satisfiable by construction.
   577	
   578	### 4.6 Masking-waste vs arena — stated as a MEASURED-LATER decision (replaces plan:293 "ablation" framing)
   579	
   580	Padded storage carries `S_max/mean_seg ≈ 16/6 ≈ 2.7×` compute/memory waste on segment-axis ops. This is **accepted for S0 and made canonical.** A flattened/arena layout (segment compaction with generations, allocation/fragmentation policy, pointer repair, graph recapture) is a **deferred optimization**, and the plan's existing padded-vs-flattened ablation (plan:293, T15) is **inverted**: it now measures whether the *flattened* layout is worth its lifecycle complexity, not whether padded is viable. The arena is adopted only if **both** hold, on telemetry: (i) the S0 profiler attributes `> 30%` of step time to segment-axis reductions (falsifier F2, plan:360), **and** (ii) the measured masking tax at H1/H2 exceeds the arena's added lifecycle cost (T15 parquet). Until both are met, `[B,S_max]`-padded is the only representation the build carries. This resolves the design/plan contradiction (design:638–652 fixed `[P,S_max]` now agrees with the plan) and removes the "padded is only an ablation" claim entirely.
   581	
   582	**Plan edits for #4:** rewrite §2.1 lines 287–293 (segment layout → `[W,N_cap,S_max]`+`seg_mask`, delete `[S_total]`/CSR/`body_id`, invert the padded/flattened framing); update §1.5 lines 165–181 (add `stable_id`/`parent_id`/`generation`, drop `body_ptr`, promote `energy`/`struct_N` to f64); update §1.6 line 201 (`DevelopedBody` → padded `[B,S_max]`); delete `numerics/reduce.py::segment_index_add` (lines 60,216,223) and rewrite plan:223 as an AST guard; rewrite `spikeswim/churn.py` note (line 99) to the §4.4 recycle-by-`cumsum` scheme.
   583	
   584	---
   585	
   700	**What Codex is right about.** `transfer(src,dst,a)` written as `src -= a; dst += a` in **f32** does *not* preserve the total: subtracting `0.5` from `1.0` is exact, but adding `0.5` to `1e8` rounds away (f32 ULP at 1e8 is ~8). The debit is realized, the credit is lost → a structural mint/leak. No compensated f64 sum *afterward* recovers information already destroyed in the f32 write. So plan:229, plan:414–425, plan:459–471 ("`residual==0`", "`<1e-9`", "million-step `<1e-6`") are unsupported **for f32 reservoir state**.
   701	
   702	**Resolution — three concrete mechanisms, all specified.**
   703	
   704	#### 5.1 Reservoir state is f64 (REPLACES plan:414 "Named f32 tensors" and the f32 rows of plan:176–177)
   705	
   706	Every *registered reservoir* — the quantity `close_books()` sums — is stored **float64**:
   707	
   708	| Reservoir tensor | shape | dtype (was → now) |
   709	|---|---|---|
   710	| `Nd, Bp, Bd, Bm` | `[W,G,G,B]` | f32 → **f64** |
   711	| `struct_N`, `energy` (reserve `E`), `mass` | `[W,N_cap]` | f32 → **f64** |
   712	| `Sed` | `[W,G,G]` | f32 → **f64** |
   713	| `R_num` (numerical-residual reservoir, §5.3) | scalar per `(W, currency)` | **f64** |
   714	
   715	The **hot physics stays f32** (swim kernel `pos/rot/vcom/M_eff/dv` per plan:174–178, §4.8). The seam: the f32 kernel *emits fluxes* (a locomotion-power draw, a drag-dissipation credit); those f32 flux amounts are cast up to f64 (an **exact** widening — f32 ⊂ f64, zero rounding) before touching any reservoir. Rationale for f64 *state* is **dynamic range, not conservation** (§5.2 already gives exact conservation): f64 keeps the residual reservoir at true machine-noise level (a small flux onto a large pool lands in the pool, not in the residual). At f64, `1e8 + 0.5` is exact (ULP ≈ 1.5e-8), so a physically-realizable flux never vanishes below the reservoir ULP unless the magnitude ratio exceeds ~1e15 — which no physical flux does.
   716	
   717	#### 5.2 The transfer primitive captures its own rounding via TwoSum (REPLACES plan:421 `transfer` and design §6.6's "index_add_ … equal credit")
   718	
   719	Even in f64, `src−a` and `dst+a` round. We make the pair **exact** with Knuth's error-free TwoSum (6 flops, branchless, correct for any magnitudes), booking the exact rounding into `R_num`:
   720	
   721	```
   722	def two_sum(x, y):            # returns (s, e) with x + y == s + e EXACTLY in f64
   723	    s = x + y
   724	    z = s - x
   725	    e = (x - (s - z)) + (y - z)
   726	    return s, e
   727	
   728	def transfer(src, dst, a, currency):     # a: f64 (f32 flux widened losslessly)
   729	    src_new, e_s = two_sum(src,  -a)      # a is masked/elementwise; deterministic, no scatter
   730	    dst_new, e_d = two_sum(dst,  +a)
   731	    src[...] = src_new
   732	    dst[...] = dst_new
   733	    R_num[currency] -= (e_s + e_d)         # the ONLY place matter/energy enters the residual pool
   734	```
   735	
   736	After the write, `src_exact + dst_exact + R_num` equals the pre-write `src + dst + R_num` **to the precision at which `R_num` itself accumulates** (O(eps²)). `R_num` is registered as a real reservoir and is included in `close_books()`. This is not a "sanctioned wrapper hiding a nondeterministic op": TwoSum is pure elementwise f64 arithmetic; the debit/credit still use the masked-axis-sum layout (canonical `[B,S_max]`+mask / grid stencils), never `scatter_add`.
   737	
   738	- **Cross-magnitude case, now correct:** `a=0.5`, `dst=1e8` (f64): `dst_new=100000000.5` exact (`e_d=0`); `src=1.0→0.5` exact (`e_s=0`); `R_num` unchanged; the 0.5 actually arrives. The f32 mint is gone.
   739	- **Sub-ULP case (only if ratio >~1e15):** `e_d≠0`, the un-representable fraction is booked to `R_num` so **nothing is minted** — and §5.4's leak guard fires if `R_num` ever grows past machine noise, surfacing the dynamic-range failure as a bug to root-cause (not a silently absorbed leak).
   740	
   741	#### 5.3 `close_books()` — exact assertion + tolerance derivation (REPLACES plan:229, plan:416/422, and the plan:459–466 thresholds)
   742	
   743	```
   744	def close_books(currency) -> LedgerResidual:
   745	    total   = pairwise_sum_f64(registered_reservoirs[currency]) + R_num[currency]   # compensated
   746	    ext     = X_in_cum[currency] - X_out_cum[currency]        # tracked external in/out (0 for S1 mass box)
   747	    res_abs = total - I0[currency] - ext
   748	    res_rel = abs(res_abs) / max(I0[currency], I_FLOOR[currency])
   749	    assert res_rel        < TAU_STEP[currency]                # bookkeeping closure
   750	    assert abs(R_num[currency]) / max(I0,I_FLOOR) < TAU_NUM_LEAK   # residual stays machine-noise
   751	    return LedgerResidual(res_abs, res_rel, R_num[currency], S_max, N_elem)
   752	```
   753	
   754	**Tolerance derivation (scale-dependent, defensible — this is the derivation Codex demanded).** The reservoirs are **all-positive** (mass and stored energy ≥ 0), so the summation condition number `κ = Σ|R_i|/|ΣR_i| = 1` — no catastrophic cancellation. A pairwise-compensated f64 reduction over `N_elem` cells then has relative error bounded by:
   755	
   756	```
   757	err_reduce ≤ C · ⌈log2 N_elem⌉ · eps_64          (C≈8 safety; eps_64 = 2.22e-16)
   758	```
   759	
   760	For the S1 box `N_elem = W·G²·B·N_res + W·N_cap = 1·256²·16·4 + 1024 ≈ 4.19e6` → `⌈log2⌉=22` → `err_reduce ≤ 8·22·2.22e-16 ≈ 3.9e-14`. Per-transfer bookkeeping error is O(eps²) (TwoSum) and does not accumulate coherently (the ±rounding random-walks, so drift is √T·eps², not T·eps). Therefore:
   761	
   762	| Tolerance | Value | Derivation |
   763	|---|---|---|
   764	| `TAU_STEP[mass]` | **1e-12** rel (f64) | `err_reduce ≈ 3.9e-14`, ×25 headroom |
   765	| `TAU_STEP[energy]` | **1e-6** rel-to-throughput (f32 fluxes) | see #15.4 — energy fluxes are f32-kernel-sourced |
   766	| drift over 1e6 steps | **< 1e-9** rel, bounded-oscillating (slope≈0 in noise) | TwoSum → O(T·eps²)≈5e-24 systematic; observed drift is reduction-noise random walk, never monotone |
   767	| `TAU_NUM_LEAK` | **1e-10** rel | `R_num` must stay machine-noise; larger ⇒ dynamic-range bug, root-cause per §1.2 |
   768	| `I_FLOOR[mass]` | 1e-6 mol | avoids divide-by-zero on an emptied box |
   769	
   770	These **supersede** plan:229 (`< 1e-9` f32), plan:459–466 (the `<1e-9 f64` rows keep their value but are now *derived*, not asserted), and design §6.6 (`τ_mass ~ 1e-9` tightens to `1e-12` on f64+TwoSum; state that the design's 1e-9 was a floor, now provably beatable).
   771	
   772	**New tests** (extend plan:459–467):
   773	
   774	```
   775	test_transfer_exact_twosum   :  after N random transfers, Σreservoirs + R_num == I0 to 1e-14 rel   [f64]
   776	test_cross_magnitude_transfer:  transfer 0.5 between a 1.0 pool and a 1e8 pool → 0.5 realized at BOTH; res_rel<1e-12  (the exact case Codex named)
   777	test_residual_stays_noise    :  1e6-step soak → |R_num|/I0 < 1e-10 throughout (dynamic-range guard)
   778	test_leak_is_caught          :  an UNPAIRED write (bypassing transfer) → close_books raises  (kept from plan:236; now also catches R_num overflow)
   779	```
   780	
   781	---
   782	
   783	### FINDING #7 — S1's bloom-and-crash is impossible with S1's processes
   784	
   785	**What Codex is right about.** As written (plan:443–444), S1 has *only* production (`Nd→Bp` until `Nd` exhausts) and remineralization/sinking of `Bd`. There is **no `Bp→Bd/Nd` producer loss**, so when `Nd` empties, production merely *stops* and `Bp` sits flat forever. "Bloom must … crash" (plan:467, plan:473) and "drifters plateau" cannot occur. `Bp` never declines; there are no consumers to plateau.
   786	
   787	**Resolution — I pick: ADD explicit producer respiration + mortality to S1** (the design already wants an in-S1 crash, §6.8: "drawdown empties `Nd` → production collapses → biomass declines"; and the remin loop that receives `Bd` already exists at S1.8). The **consumer-plateau claim moves to S3** (it genuinely needs grazing). Both moves are specified below.
   788	
   789	#### 7.1 New S1 producer-loss processes (INSERT into §S1.C, after S1.7)
   790	
   791	All units mol N; all transfers go through the §5.2 primitive; energy side per #15.
   792	
   793	```
   794	S1.7b  Producer respiration (maintenance):     Bp -> Nd,  releases heat
   795	   P_resp = m_resp · Bp                         [mol N · s⁻¹],  m_resp ≈ 0.10 /day  (anchor: phyto respiration ≈10–30% of gross production, §6.2 NPP band)
   796	   transfer(Bp, Nd, P_resp·dt, 'mass')
   797	   E_heat += e_N · P_resp·dt   ;  E_chem(Bp) tracks -e_N·P_resp·dt   (Nd carries NO energy — inorganic)
   798	
   799	S1.8b  Producer mortality (senescence + crowding):  Bp -> Bd,  energy carried
   800	   M_p = (d0 + d_dd·Bp) · Bp                     [mol N · s⁻¹],  d0 ≈ 0.05/day, d_dd density-dependent
   801	   transfer(Bp, Bd, M_p·dt, 'mass')
   802	   E_chem moves Bp->Bd with the biomass (no heat)
   803	```
   804	
   805	`d_dd·Bp` (crowding) is what gives the crash its bite: at the bloom peak `Bp` is large, mortality is super-linear, biomass collapses even as production has already stalled from `Nd` exhaustion. `M_p` feeds `Bd` → S1.8 remin returns `(1−BGE)` of it to `Nd` (deep) → S1.10 mixing lifts it → **damped re-bloom → steady standing stock**. This is genuine bloom-and-crash, fully conserved, **no cap knob** — the crash is a real loss flux, not a ceiling. `m_resp, d0, d_dd` are frozen *rate anchors* (§1.2), never retuned to hit a target biomass; a failure to stabilize is a diagnostic, not a tuning target (§6.8, §6.9).
   806	
   807	#### 7.2 S1 acceptance rewrite (REPLACES plan:473 item 3)
   808	
   809	> **3.** Blooms/deserts emerge from the loop. A seeded nutrient pulse must: (i) spike production, (ii) draw `Nd` down toward exhaustion, (iii) **crash — `Bp` rises to a peak then declines** as producer respiration+mortality (S1.7b/S1.8b) debit it faster than the stalled production replaces it, (iv) settle to a **damped oscillation / steady standing stock** as mortality→`Bd`→remin→`Nd`→mixing closes the return. **No hand-coded ceiling.** Zonation falls out of the pump. *(The consumer-plateau claim "drifters plateau at production÷intake" MOVES to S3 — it requires grazers, which S1 does not implement.)*
   810	
   811	#### 7.3 S1 test rewrites (REPLACE plan:467, ADD)
   812	
   813	```
   814	test_bloom_self_terminates (rewrite): seeded pulse → Bp(t) is unimodal-then-declining (argmax not at t_end,
   815	                                       and Bp(t_end) < Bp(peak)·0.7); Nd draws down then partially recovers;
   816	                                       close_books < 1e-12 throughout; no baseCap/carryingCap symbol reachable.
   817	test_producer_loss_closes (new):       respiration Bp->Nd and mortality Bp->Bd each pass INV-TRANSFER (|Δsrc+Δdst|<1e-14 f64);
   818	                                       energy: E_chem drop == E_heat rise (respiration) OR E_chem(Bd) rise (mortality).
   819	test_damped_steady_state (new):        1e6-step soak reaches a bounded standing stock with no cap knob; oscillation amplitude decays (not monotone growth, not zero-flat).
   820	```
   821	
   822	The plan-wide claim (plan:9, plan:473, design §6.8) "drifters plateau at production÷intake" is retagged **[S3]** everywhere it appears in S1 contexts.
   823	
   824	---
   825	
   826	### FINDING #15 — Define the full energy system as a reservoir/flux graph
   827	
   828	**What Codex is right about.** S3 debits basal metabolism + locomotion (plan:570) with **no heat/waste reservoir**; production introduces chemical energy from light while S1 models light as an *untracked analytic value* (plan:449); feeding mixes nutrient mass and energy with **no biomass energy-density conversion** (plan:578). "Energy books closed end-to-end" (plan:9, plan:558, plan:572) is rhetoric until every reservoir, flux, unit, and conversion is written down. Below is the implementable graph.
   829	
   830	#### 15.1 Conversion constants (frozen anchors, §1.2 — REPLACES the missing energy-density in plan:578)
   831	
   832	```
   833	Redfield C:N (molar)      = 106 : 16  = 6.625                          (§6.2)
   834	caloric anchor  c_calC    = 45 kJ·gC⁻¹                                 (Platt & Irwin 1973, §6.2)
   835	molar mass C    M_C       = 12.011 g·mol⁻¹
   836	⇒ energy per mol C  e_C   = 45000 · 12.011      = 5.405e5  J·(mol C)⁻¹
   837	⇒ energy per mol N  e_N   = e_C · 6.625          = 3.581e6  J·(mol N)⁻¹   ← the biomass mass↔energy conversion
   838	sim-energy anchor N_sim   = 300 J per sim-energy unit                  (§4.8, SimUnits)
   839	muscle efficiency η       = 0.20                                       (§4.8)
   840	```
   841	
   842	`e_N` is the single conversion that lets **derived** chemical energy of any N-pool be read off its mass: `E_chem(pool) = e_N · N_pool` [J]. This is a *readout*, **not a second stored-and-synced scalar** (design §6.1 — storing it independently is the very sync-glue anti-pattern P1 forbids).
   843	
   844	#### 15.2 Reservoirs (all f64, per #5) — the energy currency graph
   845	
   846	| # | Reservoir | Symbol | Unit | Stored or derived | Kind |
   847	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   848	| E1 | Producer/detritus/microbial chemical energy | `E_chem = e_N·(Bp+Bd+Bm)` | J | **derived** from mass pools | internal |
   849	| E2 | Creature metabolic reserve (lipid/glycogen) | `E_reserve = energy[W,N_cap]` | J | **stored** (§6.1: genuinely distinct) | internal |
   850	| E3 | Creature bulk kinetic energy | `E_KE = ½vᵀM_eff v` | J | derived from kernel state (f32) | internal |
   851	| E4 | Absorbed-light input meter | `E_sun_cum` | J | stored, monotone↑ (Kahan) | **INPUT** |
   852	| E5 | Heat / respiration / dissipation sink meter | `E_heat_cum` | J | stored, monotone↑ (Kahan) | **OUTPUT** |
   853	| E6 | Exported (buried) energy meter | `E_export_cum` | J | stored, monotone↑ (off at S1) | **OUTPUT** |
   854	
   855	This is an **open system**: E4 is the one external input (sun), E5+E6 the outputs. `Nd` (dissolved inorganic nutrient) and `Sed` carry **zero energy density** — re-fixing `Nd` re-injects sun energy; this decoupling is what makes the loop open, not a closed constant.
   856	
   857	#### 15.3 Fluxes (transaction equations + units; each is a §5.2 paired transfer or a metered credit)
   858	
   859	Powers in W = J·s⁻¹. `p_in` (muscle→body input power) uses the **cluster-#1-corrected** identity `p_in = tReact·U + pWake + tFin·U + pFin` (consistent with the S0 fix; the plan:345 form omitting `tFin·U` is superseded there).
   860	
   861	```
   862	F-L  Photosynthesis (INPUT):    ΔE_chem = +e_N·ΔBp ;  E_sun_cum += e_N·ΔBp
   863	        (ΔBp from S1.7 production; light energy = the energy needed to fix ΔBp from Nd. Incident I(x,z),
   864	         §3.9 Beer–Lambert, remains an analytic DRIVER, not a reservoir; only ABSORBED-and-fixed light is metered.)
   865	
   866	F-G  Grazing (chem → reserve + egesta):        [S3]
   867	        E_chem(field) -= e_N·I_bio ; E_reserve += AE·e_N·I_bio ; E_chem(Bd) += (1-AE)·e_N·I_bio
   868	        (mass side: field-=I_bio, struct_N+=AE·I_bio, Bd+=(1-AE)·I_bio ; §6.4. No heat: assimilation loss is egesta, mass-conserved.)
   869	
   870	F-M  Metabolism (reserve → heat + mech):        [S3]
   871	        P_basal = B0·M^α  (Kleiber, α≈0.79, §6.5.1)
   872	        E_reserve -= (P_basal + p_in/η)·dt
   873	        E_heat_cum += (P_basal + (1/η - 1)·p_in)·dt        # basal + muscle-inefficiency heat
   874	        E_mech_in  += p_in·dt                               # mechanical work delivered to body
   875	        (identity check: draw = basal + p_in/η = heat + mech ✓  → S3-ENERGY-2)
   876	
   877	F-K  Locomotion (mech → KE + hydro dissipation): [S0/S2 kernel]
   878	        ΔE_KE = ½vᵀM_{n+1}v_{n+1} − ½vᵀM_n v_n
   879	        E_heat_cum += (pWake + pFin + wDrag)·dt             # shed wake + fin wake + axial drag → heat
   880	        (the exact discrete balance incl. the ½vᵀ(M_{n+1}−M_n)v added-mass term is the S0 energy-gate cluster;
   881	         THIS ledger consumes the S0 StepLedger's {p_in, pWake, pFin, wDrag, ΔE_KE} as authoritative flux values.)
   882	
   883	F-R  Bacterial remineralization (chem → microbial + heat):  [S1]
   884	        R = k_remin(z)·Bd
   885	        E_chem(Bd) -= e_N·R·dt ; E_chem(Bm) += e_N·BGE·R·dt ; E_heat_cum += e_N·(1-BGE)·R·dt
   886	        (mass: BGE→Bm, (1-BGE)→Nd, §6.3. The (1-BGE) N returns to Nd but its ENERGY is respired to heat — the key decoupling.)
   887	
   888	F-P  Producer respiration + mortality:          [S1, new §7.1]
   889	        respiration: E_chem(Bp) -= e_N·P_resp·dt ; E_heat_cum += e_N·P_resp·dt
   890	        mortality:   E_chem(Bp) -= e_N·M_p·dt   ; E_chem(Bd) += e_N·M_p·dt   (no heat, biomass carries energy)
   891	
   892	F-B  Burial (OUTPUT, off at S1, on at S9):
   893	        E_export_cum += e_N·w_bury·Bd·dt          (mass: Bd→Sed; energy leaves the system)
   894	```
   895	
   896	#### 15.4 The master balance — "books close" = every flux accounted (NOT total constant)
   897	
   898	```
   899	Σ_stored(t) := E_chem(t) + E_reserve(t) + E_KE(t)
   900	INV-ENERGY:   | Σ_stored(t) − Σ_stored(0) − (E_sun_cum − E_heat_cum − E_export_cum) |  <  τ_E · (E_sun_cum + E_heat_cum + ε)
   901	```
   902	
   903	Denominator is the **cumulative throughput**, not the stored total — the correct reference for an open system whose meters grow unboundedly (this is why a raw "energy is constant" check is meaningless here). Two distinct precisions, stated honestly:
   904	
   905	- **Bookkeeping closure (each flux paired via §5.2 TwoSum):** closes to O(eps²). Catches any unpaired energy write (a mint). `τ_E_book = 1e-12`.
   906	- **Physics/work consistency (`E_chem` derived from mass; `E_KE`/`pWake`/`wDrag` from the f32 kernel):** limited to f32, `τ_E_phys = 1e-6` per step, `1e-4` drift over 1e6 steps, **gated on bounded-oscillating, not endpoint** (semi-implicit integrators oscillate — F6, §4.8). The f32-ness is a *fidelity* limit checked by the S0 oracle gate, not a conservation leak (the application is exact via TwoSum).
   907	
   908	#### 15.5 S1 / S3 energy-conservation test predicates (the deliverable Codex asked for)
   909	
   910	**S1** (abiotic + producer loop; reservoirs E1/E4/E5, no E2/E3):
   911	```
   912	S1-ENERGY-1 (bookkeeping):  | E_chem(t) − E_chem(0) − (E_sun_cum − E_heat_cum) | / (E_sun_cum+E_heat_cum+ε) < 1e-12   [f64, all fluxes TwoSum-paired]
   913	S1-ENERGY-2 (derived-not-stored):  AST/audit — E_chem is ONLY ever computed as e_N·(Bp+Bd+Bm); no independent E write to a producer pool exists (kills the sync-glue P1 anti-pattern). ⇒ mass closure (#5, 1e-12) IMPLIES energy closure for producer pools.
   914	S1-ENERGY-3 (crash is energetic):  during the §7 bloom-crash, the ΔE_chem<0 of the decline is matched cell-for-cell by E_heat_cum rise (respiration F-P/F-R) + E_chem(Bd) rise (mortality) — energy released/moved, never lost.
   915	```
   916	
   917	**S3** (full economy; all six reservoirs):
   918	```
   919	S3-ENERGY-1 (bookkeeping):  INV-ENERGY (§15.4) < 1e-9 rel-to-throughput  [mix of f64 mass-derived + f32 KE terms booked via TwoSum]
   920	S3-ENERGY-2 (metabolic identity):  per creature/step, ΔE_reserve == −(P_basal + p_in/η)·dt to 1e-6 f32; NO path banks mechanical work at implicit η=1 (design §4.8, plan:534/657).
   921	S3-ENERGY-3 (work consistency, inherited from S0):  |ΔE_KE − (p_in − (pWake+pFin+wDrag))·dt| / throughput < 1e-6 f32, bounded-oscillating over 1e5 steps.
   922	S3-ENERGY-4 (feeding closure):  e_N·I_bio == AE·e_N·I_bio (→reserve) + (1−AE)·e_N·I_bio (→Bd); INV-TRANSFER on the mass split < 1e-14 f64.
   923	S3-ENERGY-5 (no mint):  E_sun_cum is the ONLY reservoir permitted to rise without an internal debit; every other energy increase is a §5.2 paired transfer (audit test forbids raw E_i += x). ⇒ the population "sustains without a mint/cap knob" (plan:558/572) becomes a checkable predicate, not a claim.
   924	```
   925	
   926	#### 15.6 Plan edits this finding requires

   934	
   935	### Consistency notes for the re-review
   936	
   937	- **Segment layout / determinism:** every `transfer` above uses masked axis-sums on the canonical `[B,S_max]`+mask / grid stencils — no `scatter_add`, no ragged reduction (aligns with the adopted layout resolution; TwoSum is pure elementwise f64).
   938	- **f32↔f64 seam is explicit and lossless:** f32 kernel fluxes widen to f64 exactly; only f64 *reservoir writes* round, and TwoSum captures that into `R_num`. This is the "explicit numerical-residual reservoir that absorbs any f32↔f64 transfer rounding" the cross-cutting resolution mandates.
   939	- **`p_in` uses the cluster-#1-corrected identity** (`+ tFin·U`) everywhere it appears (F-K, F-M, S3-ENERGY-3), so the energy system and the S0 gate cannot disagree.
   940	- **Anchors are frozen, not tuned** (§1.2): `e_N, c_calC, η, N_sim, m_resp, d0, k_remin, BGE` are measurements; a non-closing ledger or an implausible equilibrium is root-caused upstream, never softened by retuning an anchor or re-hiding a mint (§6.8–6.9).

  1373	
  1374	Notes that make this hold up under harsh re-review:
  1375	- **`allow_indirect_imports = True`** on contracts 2–5 is load-bearing: it means only a *direct* `import sirrobin.fields.nutrient` from `core` fails, while the legitimate chain `core → fields.contracts → (internally) fields.nutrient` passes. Without it every contracts module would trip its own contract.
  1376	- The `layers` contract (1) already forbids *upward* imports, so lower layers are not listed as sources in the internals contracts (e.g. `physics` is never a source against `fields`, because `physics→fields` is already a layers violation). Sources are only the *strictly higher* layers.
  1377	- Contract 6 uses the `.*` one-segment wildcard alongside the bare package name to catch both the package and its submodules (import-linter 2.1). The internals contracts (2–5) **enumerate** rather than wildcard, because `forbidden` has no exclusion syntax and `contracts.py` must stay importable — enumeration is the only way to allow exactly `L.contracts` while blocking every sibling.
  1378	
  1379	### 18.3 Enumeration-drift guard (REPLACES the belt-and-braces note at plan:142 and hardens plan:106)
  1380	
  1381	Because contracts 2–5 enumerate current modules, a *new* private module added later would silently escape the firewall. Close that procedurally in `tests/test_import_boundary.py`:
  1382	
  1383	```python
  1384	def test_every_private_module_is_firewalled():
  1385	    # For each layer L in {physics, fields, genetics, core}: every .py in L
  1386	    # except contracts.py and __init__.py MUST appear in that layer's
  1387	    # *-internals-private forbidden_modules list in setup.cfg.
  1388	    # Fails CI when a new internal module is added but not registered.
  1389	```
  1390	
  1391	Plus the existing programmatic run of all six contracts via `import-linter`'s API so a violation fails an ordinary `pytest` (not just the `lint-imports` CI job), and an assertion of interface opacity (INV-W4): no `plate`/`seed`/`hotspot`/`octave`/`swim_step`/`nutrient` symbol is reachable from a consumer through anything but a `*.contracts` module. CI job `boundary` (plan:240) runs `lint-imports --config setup.cfg` and this test, fail-fast, before `conservation`.
  1392	
  1393	**G‑SCAF‑2 (plan:262) is strengthened:** the injected-violation probe must now include, in addition to `physics→core`: (a) `core→physics.swim_step` (concrete-internal reach), (b) `observe→core.colony` (top layer reaching a concrete), and (c) `core→fields.nutrient` (the previously-unguarded field internal) — each must independently fail `lint-imports`.

codex
## Verdicts on the original 20 findings

1. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 replaces the invalid KE equation with separate force-law identities and a discrete constrained-update residual, which is real progress ([Rev-2 lines 48–91](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:48)). But the fin identity correctly requires `Ucl=max(0,U)` while the combined identity reverts to signed `tFin·U`, and the later S3 gate again asserts the invalid `ΔKE=(p_in−wake−drag)dt`, omitting both `½vᵀΔMv` and the tail-tip/COM-velocity distinction.

2. **PARTIAL.** The 2×2 constrained x/z solve and explicit `J_y` are concrete and physically preferable to 3×3-then-zero ([Rev-2 lines 93–120](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:93)). But the production solver later adds unledgered Tikhonov mass; once regularization fires, the stated equation `MΔv=P+J_c` is false, so the reaction and energy proof no longer describe the implemented solve.

3. **RESOLVED.** Fixed `[B,S_max]` storage turns the missing ragged algorithm into an explicit masked axis reduction, deletes duplicate-destination scatter, and adds both graph auditing and repeatability tests ([Rev-2 lines 245–289](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:245)). That is a concrete reduction mechanism, not a renamed atomic wrapper.

4. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 chooses fixed padded storage and gives static birth/death operations, eliminating the fundamental `[S_total]` contradiction ([Rev-2 lines 466–580](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:466)). It then defines mutually incompatible layouts: `S_max=16` real physics segments with root self-parent here, versus slot 0 reserved as a masked sentinel and real segments only in `[1,S_max)` under #9—leaving capacity 15 and two different parent conventions.

5. **STILL-BROKEN.** Moving reservoirs to f64 is sound, but the proposed TwoSum correction has the wrong sign: because `x+y=s+e`, closure with `total=sum(stored)+R_num` requires `R_num += e_s+e_d`, not `-=` ([Rev-2 lines 717–745](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:717)). The exact mechanism intended to close sub-ULP transfers instead doubles the discrepancy.

6. **PARTIAL.** The delta derives targets from a stated 12-hour run, scopes S0 to locomotion only, and creates an actual whole-tick gate ([Rev-2 lines 951–1010](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:951)). But G-E2E is described as “post-S2” while requiring S3 feeding/metabolism, and the S0 headroom double-counts population growth even though the derivation already uses worst-case `N_pop≈N_cap`.

7. **RESOLVED.** Producer respiration and mortality provide concrete `Bp→Nd` and `Bp→Bd` loss paths, while the consumer plateau is correctly deferred to S3 ([Rev-2 lines 783–822](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:783)). The exact transient remains parameter-dependent, but the originally impossible crash is now mechanically possible.

8. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 commits to an untouched gain0 donor and independent analytic gain1 fixtures, but never specifies the gain1 fixture construction beyond naming mass, Lamb factors, force, and momentum. Meanwhile bug-active H1/H2 fixtures are regenerated from a patched donor, so broad conformance still risks port-versus-self-authored-oracle circularity rather than an independently calculated expected result.

9. **PARTIAL.** The sentinel-row scheme fixes unsafe root and empty-tail gathers and adds the requested test matrix ([Rev-2 lines 122–142](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:122)). It conflicts with #4’s root-self-parent layout and reduces the advertised 16-segment capacity to 15; moreover, the zero-mass empty body still enters a solver that produces `1/0` and then `0·∞`.

10. **RESOLVED.** `quat_inv=conj(q)/‖q‖²`, donor normalization policy, denormalized-quaternion tests, and a long-chain oracle test directly address the mismatch ([Rev-2 lines 145–168](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:145)). The zero-quaternion branch should be included in the Unity comparison corpus, but it is unreachable under the stated valid-body construction.

11. **RESOLVED.** The hot-loop tensor set is explicitly f32, Lamb coefficients are cast after f64 construction, validation gets a separate f64 configuration, and the throughput number is read only from f32 rows ([Rev-2 lines 171–185](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:171)). That closes the accidental-promotion hole in S0.

12. **PARTIAL.** The extreme-morphology corpus, compile-parity test, donor/production solver split, and scale-relative intent are concrete ([Rev-2 lines 187–220](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:187)). The alleged robust solver still uses cancellation-prone `λ_min=(tr−disc)/2`, has no valid `tr=0` branch, silently replaces bad determinants with a positive floor, and changes the dynamics through unaccounted regularization.

13. **PARTIAL.** A counter-addressed Philox design, stable IDs, allocator state, and checkpoint fields genuinely replace sequential-stream fragility ([Rev-2 lines 322–430](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:322)). But #13 assigns IDs from per-world `next_eid[W]` while the key omits `world_id`, causing identical RNG addresses across worlds; #4/#14 instead specify one global host allocator, so there is no authoritative implementation.

14. **PARTIAL.** `SimulationSnapshot` is a materially complete checkpoint concept and the positive round-trip continuation test is appropriate ([Rev-2 lines 586–676](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:586)). It duplicates `energy` and `struct_N` in both `colony` and `reservoirs`, disagrees with #13 about allocator shape, omits future external actions needed for “snapshot alone” replay, and requires `safetensors` without adding it to the exact dependency pins.

15. **STILL-BROKEN.** The reservoir table and flux graph are detailed, but the creature’s structural chemical energy is absent from `Σ_stored`: grazing credits `struct_N` and reserve simultaneously, then death can convert that unaccounted `struct_N` into energetic `Bd`, minting `e_N·struct_N` ([Rev-2 lines 844–900](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:844)). Worse, S3-ENERGY-3 reinstates precisely the invalid whole-body equation #1 rejected ([Rev-2 lines 917–923](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:917)).

16. **RESOLVED.** The artifact-level dependency table, regenerated graph, and critical-path text consistently make S4 and S5 parallel children of S3 and S6 dependent only on S1 fields ([Rev-2 lines 1116–1180](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:1116)). This directly removes the three conflicting dependency narratives.

17. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 moves asexual mutation/inheritance/lineage into S3, trims S4 sensing to available fields, and makes seeded predation the engineering gate ([Rev-2 lines 1182–1247](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:1182)). But mutation rates remain unnamed config values, the text incorrectly says genotype mutation preserves `S_max` rather than `N_max/E_max`, and it uses `PARAM_MUT`/`STRUCT_TOGGLE` event kinds absent from the canonical RNG enum.

18. **PARTIAL.** The exhaustive contracts and enumeration-drift test are much stronger than v1 ([Rev-2 lines 1256–1393](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:1256)). However, #14 adds `core/snapshot.py` while the core-private forbidden list omits it, so the proposed drift test fails the proposed configuration immediately; the S7 viewer’s required snapshot access is also not exposed through `core.contracts`.

19. **PARTIAL.** The staged 35–40-cell funnel, repetition policy, cache isolation, timeout, synchronization, and profiling limits are concrete enough to replace the 576-cell explosion. It still contains an unresolved `vram_cap="<PIN Q#1>"`, requires building a flattened prototype despite declaring that representation deferred, and leaves compile warmup failure behavior unspecified ([Rev-2 lines 1013–1060](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:1013)).

20. **PARTIAL.** The register now elevates energy correctness, end-to-end throughput, oracle independence, and S1 realizability. It nevertheless marks churn/static capture and conservation “RETIRED” even though the birth gather is unsafe, the canonical layouts conflict, and the residual correction has the wrong sign ([Rev-2 lines 1063–1097](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:1063)).

## New problems introduced by Rev-2

- **The lifecycle gather repeats the original eager-gather bug.** `free_rank` is `-1` for occupied slots before the first free slot, yet `torch.gather(payload_field,…free_rank…)` evaluates for every slot before `where` masks it ([lines 546–559](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:546)). Indices must be clamped/remapped before gathering.

- **The empty-body solver is not finite.** With `M00=M02=M22=0`, `tr=0`, the fallback determinant is also zero, `inv=∞`, and the zero numerator produces NaN. This directly falsifies #9’s promised empty-body behavior.

- **Regularization invalidates both KKT mechanics and the energy gate.** Production solves `(M+λI)Δv=P`, while the ledger claims `MΔv=P+J_c`. Either ledger the regularization impulse/work explicitly or do not call the balance exact.

- **The stable-ID/RNG specifications are mutually exclusive.** Per-world IDs without a world key collide; the alternative global host counter breaks the stated all-device allocator and differs from the snapshot schema. Pick one: preferably include `world_id` in the counter and retain `next_eid[W]` on-device.

- **The Philox implementation makes a false arithmetic claim.** A 32×32 unsigned product can require 64 unsigned bits and does not “fit in int64 exactly”; the implementation relies on signed overflow/wrap and arithmetic shift semantics. That behavior must be proven against reference vectors under eager and compiled CPU/CUDA, not described as ordinary exact int64 arithmetic.

- **Distribution transforms are underspecified.** Box–Muller receives uniforms in `[0,1)`, so `u=0` permits `log(0)`, and rejection sampling has only an 8-bit `draw_index` with no exhaustion rule.

- **The energy graph omits structural-body chemical energy.** This is not a tolerance issue; it is an actual mint on assimilation/death. The plan must decide whether assimilated biomass energy resides in `struct_N`, reserve, or a specified split and include it exactly once.

- **The mechanics and ecological energy definitions contradict each other.** #1 correctly says COM thrust work is not `p_in` and requires the `ΔM` term; #15 later asserts the opposite. There is no single authoritative energy equation.

- **The f64 policy is internally inconsistent.** #11 advertises only O(W) f64 runtime islands, while #5 makes every multi-million-cell field reservoir and transfer f64. That may be defensible, but it is a major whole-tick performance cost that G-E2E must measure, not a “cheap global ledger.”

- **The snapshot has duplicate authoritative state.** `energy` and `struct_N` appear in both `colony` and `reservoirs`; restore behavior on disagreement is undefined. A checkpoint cannot have two sources of truth.

- **The import firewall cannot pass its own drift guard.** `core/snapshot.py` is added in #14 but absent from #18’s enumerated core internals.

## Overall verdict

**Not approvable to begin S0.**

The immediate blockers are:

1. Fix the TwoSum residual sign and specify deterministic reduction of tensor error terms.
2. Publish one canonical segment layout: either 16 real slots plus a separate sentinel, or 15 real slots—along with one root-parent convention.
3. Add explicit finite handling for empty/zero-mass bodies.
4. Replace or repair the constrained solver so regularization is reflected in momentum and energy accounting.
5. Reconcile `U` versus `Ucl` and delete the contradictory S3 energy equation.
6. Choose one stable-ID allocator and make RNG keys unique across worlds.
7. Supply an actual independent gain1 fixture specification.
8. Fix the unsafe lifecycle gathers and pin the benchmark hardware/VRAM.

Rev-2 is substantially more concrete than v1, but its remaining failures are in the exact gates meant to authorize S0: layout, finite mechanics, energy accounting, determinism under churn, and oracle independence. Starting implementation now would encode contradictions rather than resolve them.

## Verdicts on the original 20 findings

1. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 replaces the invalid KE equation with separate force-law identities and a discrete constrained-update residual, which is real progress ([Rev-2 lines 48–91](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:48)). But the fin identity correctly requires `Ucl=max(0,U)` while the combined identity reverts to signed `tFin·U`, and the later S3 gate again asserts the invalid `ΔKE=(p_in−wake−drag)dt`, omitting both `½vᵀΔMv` and the tail-tip/COM-velocity distinction.

2. **PARTIAL.** The 2×2 constrained x/z solve and explicit `J_y` are concrete and physically preferable to 3×3-then-zero ([Rev-2 lines 93–120](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:93)). But the production solver later adds unledgered Tikhonov mass; once regularization fires, the stated equation `MΔv=P+J_c` is false, so the reaction and energy proof no longer describe the implemented solve.

3. **RESOLVED.** Fixed `[B,S_max]` storage turns the missing ragged algorithm into an explicit masked axis reduction, deletes duplicate-destination scatter, and adds both graph auditing and repeatability tests ([Rev-2 lines 245–289](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:245)). That is a concrete reduction mechanism, not a renamed atomic wrapper.

4. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 chooses fixed padded storage and gives static birth/death operations, eliminating the fundamental `[S_total]` contradiction ([Rev-2 lines 466–580](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:466)). It then defines mutually incompatible layouts: `S_max=16` real physics segments with root self-parent here, versus slot 0 reserved as a masked sentinel and real segments only in `[1,S_max)` under #9—leaving capacity 15 and two different parent conventions.

5. **STILL-BROKEN.** Moving reservoirs to f64 is sound, but the proposed TwoSum correction has the wrong sign: because `x+y=s+e`, closure with `total=sum(stored)+R_num` requires `R_num += e_s+e_d`, not `-=` ([Rev-2 lines 717–745](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:717)). The exact mechanism intended to close sub-ULP transfers instead doubles the discrepancy.

6. **PARTIAL.** The delta derives targets from a stated 12-hour run, scopes S0 to locomotion only, and creates an actual whole-tick gate ([Rev-2 lines 951–1010](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:951)). But G-E2E is described as “post-S2” while requiring S3 feeding/metabolism, and the S0 headroom double-counts population growth even though the derivation already uses worst-case `N_pop≈N_cap`.

7. **RESOLVED.** Producer respiration and mortality provide concrete `Bp→Nd` and `Bp→Bd` loss paths, while the consumer plateau is correctly deferred to S3 ([Rev-2 lines 783–822](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:783)). The exact transient remains parameter-dependent, but the originally impossible crash is now mechanically possible.

8. **PARTIAL.** Rev-2 commits to an untouched gain0 donor and independent analytic gain1 fixtures, but never specifies the gain1 fixture construction beyond naming mass, Lamb factors, force, and momentum. Meanwhile bug-active H1/H2 fixtures are regenerated from a patched donor, so broad conformance still risks port-versus-self-authored-oracle circularity rather than an independently calculated expected result.

9. **PARTIAL.** The sentinel-row scheme fixes unsafe root and empty-tail gathers and adds the requested test matrix ([Rev-2 lines 122–142](/mnt/c/Users/cddal/SirRobin/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-11-sirrobin-plan-rev2-corrections.md:122)). The zero-quaternion branch should be included in the Unity comparison corpus, but it is unreachable under the stated valid-body construction.
